[tool-efa] read_file ->   1612			this.ext(rt, rs, pos, size);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1613		    } else {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1614			throw new Error(`Unknown SPECIAL3 function: 0x${function_field.toString(16)}`);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1615		    }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1616		    break;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1617		}        
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1618	        
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1619	      case 0x1C: // SPECIAL2
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1620	        {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1621	          const rs = (instruction >> 21) & 0x1F;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1622	          const rt = (instruction >> 16) & 0x1F;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1623	          const rd = (instruction >> 11) & 0x1F;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1624	          const function_field = instruction & 0x3F;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1625	          
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1626	          switch (function_field) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1627	            case 0x02: this.mul(rs, rt, rd); break; // MUL
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1628		    case 0x20: this.clz(rs, rd); break;     // CLZ
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1629	          default:
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1630		      console.log("Uknown", instruction.toString(16));
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1631	              throw new Error(`Unknown SPECIAL2 function: 0x${function_field.toString(16)}`);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1632	          }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1633	        }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1634	        break;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1635	        
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1636	      default:
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1637	        throw new Error(`Unknown opcode: 0x${opcode.toString(16)}`);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1638	    }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1639	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1640	    // Always ensure register $zero is 0
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1641	    this.registers[0] = 0;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1642	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1643	    return 0; // Continue execution
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1644	  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1645	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1646	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1647	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1648	  // Method to run a program
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1649	  runProgram(startAddress) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1650	    this.pc = startAddress >>> 0; // Force 32-bit unsigned
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1651	    let instructionCount = 0;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1652	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1653	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1654	      let counter = 0;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1655		try {  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1656	      while (1) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1657		  const instruction = this.memory[this.pc] || 0;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1658	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1659	          const oldPc = this.pc;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1660	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1661	          this.pc = (this.pc + 4) >>> 0; // Increment PC before execution
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1662	        
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1663	          const result = this.runInstruction(instruction);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1664	          instructionCount++;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1665	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1666		  if (this.pc != ((oldPc + 4) >>> 0)) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1667		      const newPc = this.pc;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1668		      this.pc = ((oldPc + 8) >>> 0)
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1669	              const delaySlotInstruction = this.memory[oldPc + 4] || 0;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1670	              this.runInstruction(delaySlotInstruction);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1671		      this.pc = newPc;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1672		      instructionCount++;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1673		  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1674	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1675	          
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1676	        // Check for program termination conditions
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1677	        if (result !== 0 || this.pc === 0 || this.pc === oldPc) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1678	          console.log(`Program terminated at PC=0x${this.pc.toString(16)}`);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1679	          break;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1680	        }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1681	      }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1682	      
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1683	      console.log(`Executed ${instructionCount} instructions`);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1684	      return this.registers[2]; // Return value in $v0
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1685	    } catch (error) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1686	      console.error(`Execution error at PC=0x${this.pc.toString(16)}: ${error.message}`);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1687	      return -1;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1688	    }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1689	  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1690	}
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1691	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1692	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1693	
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1694	// ELF Constants
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1695	const ELF_CLASS_32 = 1;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1696	const ELF_CLASS_64 = 2;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1697	const ELF_DATA_2_LSB = 1; // Little endian
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1698	const ELF_DATA_2_MSB = 2; // Big endian
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1699	// Section types
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1700	const SHT_SYMTAB = 2;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1701	const SHT_STRTAB = 3;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1702	// Symbol types
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1703	const STT_FUNC = 2; // Function symbol
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1704	// Symbol info macros
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1705	const STT_TYPE = value => value & 0xf;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1706	/**
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1707	 * Parse an ELF file and extract memory, symbols, and function information
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1708	 * @param {string} elfPath - Path to the ELF file
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1709	 */
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1710	function runElf(elfPath) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1711	  const memory = {};
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1712	  const symbols = {};
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1713	  const invSymbols = {};
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1714	  const addrToFn = {};
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1715	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1716	  // Read the ELF file
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1717	  const fileBuffer = fs.readFileSync(elfPath);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1718	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1719	  // Parse ELF header
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1720	  const elfHeader = parseElfHeader(fileBuffer);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1721	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1722	  // Parse section headers
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1723	  const sectionHeaders = parseSectionHeaders(fileBuffer, elfHeader);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1724	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1725	  // Get section header string table
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1726	  const shstrtab = getSectionData(fileBuffer, sectionHeaders[elfHeader.shstrndx]);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1727	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1728	  // Find relevant sections
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1729	  let textSection = null;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1730	  let dataSection = null;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1731	  let rodataSection = null;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1732	  let symbolTableSections = [];
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1733	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1734	  for (let i = 0; i < sectionHeaders.length; i++) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1735	    const section = sectionHeaders[i];
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1736	    const sectionName = getString(shstrtab, section.name);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1737	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1738	    if (sectionName === '.text') {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1739	      textSection = section;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1740	    } else if (sectionName === '.data') {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1741	      dataSection = section;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1742	    } else if (sectionName === '.rodata') {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1743	      rodataSection = section;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1744	    } else if (section.type === SHT_SYMTAB) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1745	      symbolTableSections.push(section);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1746	    }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1747	  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1748	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1749	  // Load sections into memory
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1750	  const sectionsToLoad = [textSection, dataSection, rodataSection].filter(s => s !== null);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1751	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1752	  for (const section of sectionsToLoad) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1753	    const vaddr = section.addr;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1754	    const size = section.size;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1755	    const data = getSectionData(fileBuffer, section);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1756	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1757	    // Load the section data into memory (assuming 4-byte words)
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1758	    for (let i = 0; i < data.length; i += 4) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1759	      if (i + 4 <= data.length) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1760	        // For MIPS, we use little-endian byte order
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1761	        const word = data.readUInt32LE(i);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1762	        memory[vaddr + i] = word;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1763	      }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1764	    }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1765	  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1766	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1767	  let entryPoint = 0; // Default entry point
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1768	  const functionBounds = [];
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1769	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1770	  // Parse symbol tables
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1771	  for (const symbolTableSection of symbolTableSections) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1772	    // Get the associated string table
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1773	    const strTabIdx = symbolTableSection.link;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1774	    const strTabSection = sectionHeaders[strTabIdx];
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1775	    const strTab = getSectionData(fileBuffer, strTabSection);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1776	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1777	    // Parse symbols
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1778	    const entrySize = symbolTableSection.entsize;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1779	    const symbolData = getSectionData(fileBuffer, symbolTableSection);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1780	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1781	    for (let offset = 0; offset < symbolData.length; offset += entrySize) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1782	      const symbol = parseSymbol(symbolData, offset, elfHeader.class);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1783	      const symbolName = getString(strTab, symbol.name);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1784	      
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1785	      if (symbolName) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1786	        symbols[symbolName] = symbol.value;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1787	        invSymbols[symbol.value] = symbolName;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1788	        
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1789	        if (STT_TYPE(symbol.info) === STT_FUNC) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1790	          functionBounds.push([symbol.value, symbol.size, symbolName]);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1791	          
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1792	          if (symbolName === 'main') {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1793	            entryPoint = symbol.value;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1794	          }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1795	        }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1796	      }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1797	    }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1798	  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1799	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1800	  // Sort function bounds by address
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1801	  functionBounds.sort((a, b) => a[0] - b[0]);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1802	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1803	  // Create mapping from each instruction address to its function
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1804	  for (const [startAddr, size, funcName] of functionBounds) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1805	    const endAddr = startAddr + size;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1806	    let currAddr = startAddr;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1807	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1808	    while (currAddr < endAddr) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1809	      if (currAddr in memory) { // Only map addresses that exist in memory
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1810	        addrToFn[currAddr] = funcName;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1811	      }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1812	      currAddr += 4;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1813	    }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1814	  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1815	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1816	  // Create a proper JSON object with keys as strings
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1817	  const memoryOutput = {};
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1818	  for (const [addr, value] of Object.entries(memory)) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1819	    memoryOutput[addr] = value;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1820	  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1821	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1822	  // Output as valid JSON
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1823	    return {"memory": memoryOutput,
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1824		    "entry_point": entryPoint
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1825		   }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1826	}
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1827	/**
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1828	 * Parse the ELF header
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1829	 * @param {Buffer} buffer - The ELF file buffer
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1830	 * @returns {Object} The parsed ELF header
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1831	 */
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1832	function parseElfHeader(buffer) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1833	  // Check ELF magic number
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1834	  if (buffer.readUInt32BE(0) !== 0x7F454C46) { // 0x7F + "ELF"
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1835	    throw new Error('Not a valid ELF file');
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1836	  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1837	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1838	  const elfClass = buffer[4]; // 1 = 32-bit, 2 = 64-bit
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1839	  const elfData = buffer[5];  // 1 = little-endian, 2 = big-endian
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1840	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1841	  const isLittleEndian = elfData === ELF_DATA_2_LSB;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1842	  const readHalf = isLittleEndian ? buffer.readUInt16LE : buffer.readUInt16BE;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1843	  const readWord = isLittleEndian ? buffer.readUInt32LE : buffer.readUInt32BE;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1844	  const readAddr = elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1845	    ? (isLittleEndian ? buffer.readBigUInt64LE : buffer.readBigUInt64BE)
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1846	    : readWord;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1847	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1848	  const headerSize = elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 64 : 52;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1849	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1850	  return {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1851	    class: elfClass,
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1852	    data: elfData,
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1853	    version: buffer[6],
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1854	    osabi: buffer[7],
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1855	    abiversion: buffer[8],
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1856	    type: readHalf.call(buffer, 16),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1857	    machine: readHalf.call(buffer, 18),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1858	    version2: readWord.call(buffer, 20),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1859	    entry: Number(readAddr.call(buffer, 24)),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1860	    phoff: Number(readAddr.call(buffer, elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 32 : 28)),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1861	    shoff: Number(readAddr.call(buffer, elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 40 : 32)),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1862	    flags: readWord.call(buffer, elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 48 : 36),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1863	    ehsize: readHalf.call(buffer, elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 52 : 40),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1864	    phentsize: readHalf.call(buffer, elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 54 : 42),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1865	    phnum: readHalf.call(buffer, elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 56 : 44),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1866	    shentsize: readHalf.call(buffer, elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 58 : 46),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1867	    shnum: readHalf.call(buffer, elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 60 : 48),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1868	    shstrndx: readHalf.call(buffer, elfClass === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 62 : 50),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1869	    isLittleEndian
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1870	  };
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1871	}
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1872	/**
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1873	 * Parse section headers
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1874	 * @param {Buffer} buffer - The ELF file buffer
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1875	 * @param {Object} elfHeader - The parsed ELF header
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1876	 * @returns {Array} Array of section headers
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1877	 */
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1878	function parseSectionHeaders(buffer, elfHeader) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1879	  const sectionHeaders = [];
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1880	  const isLittleEndian = elfHeader.isLittleEndian;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1881	  const readWord = isLittleEndian ? buffer.readUInt32LE : buffer.readUInt32BE;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1882	  const readXword = elfHeader.class === ELF_CLASS_64
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1883	    ? (isLittleEndian ? buffer.readBigUInt64LE : buffer.readBigUInt64BE)
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1884	    : readWord;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1885	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1886	  const sectionHeaderSize = elfHeader.shentsize;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1887	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1888	  for (let i = 0; i < elfHeader.shnum; i++) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1889	    const offset = elfHeader.shoff + i * sectionHeaderSize;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1890	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1891	    const section = {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1892	      name: readWord.call(buffer, offset),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1893	      type: readWord.call(buffer, offset + 4),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1894	      flags: Number(readXword.call(buffer, offset + 8)),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1895	      addr: Number(readXword.call(buffer, offset + (elfHeader.class === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 16 : 12))),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1896	      offset: Number(readXword.call(buffer, offset + (elfHeader.class === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 24 : 16))),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1897	      size: Number(readXword.call(buffer, offset + (elfHeader.class === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 32 : 20))),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1898	      link: readWord.call(buffer, offset + (elfHeader.class === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 40 : 24)),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1899	      info: readWord.call(buffer, offset + (elfHeader.class === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 44 : 28)),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1900	      addralign: Number(readXword.call(buffer, offset + (elfHeader.class === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 48 : 32))),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1901	      entsize: Number(readXword.call(buffer, offset + (elfHeader.class === ELF_CLASS_64 ? 56 : 36)))
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1902	    };
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1903	    
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1904	    sectionHeaders.push(section);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1905	  }
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1906	  
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1907	  return sectionHeaders;
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1908	}
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1909	/**
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1910	 * Get section data
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1911	 * @param {Buffer} buffer - The ELF file buffer
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1912	 * @param {Object} section - The section header
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1913	 * @returns {Buffer} The section data
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1914	 */
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1915	function getSectionData(buffer, section) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1916	  return buffer.slice(section.offset, section.offset + section.size);
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1917	}
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1918	/**
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1919	 * Parse a symbol from a symbol table
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1920	 * @param {Buffer} data - The symbol table data
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1921	 * @param {number} offset - Offset in the symbol table
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1922	 * @param {number} elfClass - ELF class (32 or 64 bit)
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1923	 * @returns {Object} The parsed symbol
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1924	 */
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1925	function parseSymbol(data, offset, elfClass) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1926	  if (elfClass === ELF_CLASS_32) {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1927	    return {
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1928	      name: data.readUInt32LE(offset),
[tool-efa] read_file ->   1929	      value: data.readUInt32LE(offset + 4),

The current build uses PIC (position-independent code) with `addu gp,gp,t9`. The context says the VM starts at `main` with `$t9=0`, so the `addu gp,gp,t9` computes the wrong `$gp` — but actually since t9=0, gp = 0x0011337c which is the start of the .got section's gp-relative base (gp = 0x11337c). Wait, .got is at 0x0050b840. gp base = 0x00400000 + ... hmm. Actually gp for MIPS o32 is conventionally `.got` + 0x7ff0 or something. Here `lui gp,0x11; addiu gp,gp,0x337c` => gp = 0x0011337c + t9. If t9=0, gp = 0x0011337c, which isn't the real gp. Proper symbolic value would point to 0x0050b840 - 0x7ff0 = 0x00503850 or similar... Hmm.

Let me think about what the actual computed gp should be. `lui $gp, 0x11; addiu $gp, $gp, 0x337c` = 0x0011337c. Then the addiu gp,gp,t9 adds t9. When t9 is the entry point of the function (e.g., main at 0x4004b4, or __start at 0x4004f0 for __start's prologue), then gp = 0x0011337c + 0x4004b4 = 0x514830 which is near... hmm. That seems wrong too? Let me think.

Wait, actually there's a subtlety: when the compiler generates `_gp` based code with `-fPIC`, the prologue is:
```
lui gp, %hi(_gp)
addiu gp, gp, %lo(_gp)
addu gp, gp, t9
```
The `%hi(_gp)` / `%lo(_gp)` refer to the `_gp` symbol which is specifically set to `_GLOBAL_OFFSET_TABLE_ - 0x7ff0`. So gp computed = _gp + t9.

For the library `__start` at 0x4004f0, t9 = 0x4004f0 => gp = 0x11337c + 0x4004f0 = 0x51386c. Hmm.

Let me check what `_gp` is: 0x0011337c. That's in the middle of .rodata (0x4b3d50..0x4e56e0). Hmm. That seems odd. Unless this binary was built with unusual section addresses-through vma adjustments.

Anyway the main issue from the summary: The VM doesn't load `.got`/`.sdata`/`.sbss` and VM starts at `main` with `$t9=0`, so PIC prologues compute wrong `$gp` (lui/addiu/addu). The fix we need: build the binary so it doesn't rely on PIC, or make it work with the VM's constraints.

Actually, before diving into debugging, let me think about the whole approach.

The challenge: Build a MIPS ELF that runs correctly in an unusual VM with:
1. Loads only `.text`, `.data`, `.rodata` sections (as little-endian words).

So VM initial state: sp = 0x7FFFFFFC, all regs 0, memory = only .text/.data/.rodata words.

Now the code compiled for MIPS o32 PIC: each function begins with a gp prologue:
```
lui gp, %hi(_gp)
addiu gp, gp, %lo(_gp)
addu gp, gp, t9
```
The `t9` value is the address of the function (for local calls, $t9 is set to the address of the callee by the caller's `bal`/`jal`). Since the VM starts at `main` directly without setting t9, t9=0, so gp = _gp constant = 0x0011337c. But the actual gp needed is `_gp` + function_address.

Actually wait, in MIPS PIC code, when a function is called, $t9 is set to the function's entry address (for global functions) so that the PIC prologue can compute gp = _gp + t9. For a local static function called within the same object, the usual convention... hmm actually for static functions, the compiler can still generate the gp setup based on the function's own address. Let me not overthink.

For a static non-PIC MIPS build, the compiler will place globals in `.data`/`.bss`/`.sdata`/`.sbss` with direct addressing (e.g., `lui a0, %hi(x); lw a0, %lo(x)(a0)`). If we extend .data's size to cover from 0x4fbb90 to 0x50d6cc, we'd also need the file offsets to be contiguous (they are: .data offset 0xebb90, __libc_IO_vtables at 0x50b4d0 off 0xfb4d0, __libc_atexit 0x50b818 off 0xfb818, __libc_subfreeres 0x50b81c off 0xfb81c, .got 0x50b840 off 0xfb840, .bss 0x50d6d0 off 0xfd6cc NOBITS). Total from .data offset (0xebb90) to end of .got (0xfb840+0x1e8c = 0xfd6cc) = 0xfd6cc - 0xebb90 = 0x11b3c bytes. The intermediate sections' data is in the file, contiguous.

So if we create a new section (call it ".data" extended) covering vaddr 0x4fbb90..0x50d6cc with file offset 0xebb90..0xfd6cc, the VM would load all of .data + vtables + atexit + subfreeres + .got into memory at their proper vaddrs, since they are contiguous in both vaddr and file offset.

The VM's `bgezal` doesn't set $t9. It only sets $ra. So when the PIC call loads `t9` from the GOT (`lw t9, offset(gp)`) and does `bgezal`/bal, the callee's prologue does `addu gp,gp,t9` — with t9 loaded from the GOT as the callee's address. That's correct IF the GOT is loaded and gp is correct.

So the concern about t9=0 applies only to the entry point (main), since the VM starts at main without loading t9. For all internal function calls, the code loads t9 from the GOT and passes it through the `bal` (bgezal) — the VM's bgezal doesn't clobber t9, so t9 remains the loaded callee address. Good.

Therefore the plan for the current PIC build with the current /app/vm.js:
- Problem (a): .got not loaded → fix by extending `.data` (or creating a merged section) so the VM loads .got content.
- Problem (b): main's prologue computes wrong gp because t9=0 at entry → patch main's prologue's lui/addiu to hardcode the correct gp value (0x513830) and/or make `addu gp,gp,t9` a no-op.

Actually with t9=0, `addu gp, gp, t9` computes gp = 0x11337c + 0 = 0x11337c if we only fix lui/addiu to load 0x513830... wait if we change lui/addiu to produce 0x513830, then addu gp,gp,t9 with t9=0 leaves gp=0x513830. 

But we must be careful: internal function calls set t9 to the callee address before `bal`. When we patch main's prologue, that only affects main. Other functions still compute gp = _gp + t9 correctly (because t9 is set by the caller via `lw t9, offset(gp)` + `bal`).

Actually alternatively — since we're patching binaries anyway, maybe it's easier to just merge .data and .got bytes into a single section in the ELF. Let me write a Python post-processor that:
- Reads the ELF.
- Identifies .text, .data, .rodata, .got (and any other alloc sections needed, e.g., .sdata/.sbss if present, __libc_IO_vtables etc).
- Builds a list of "loadable" sections: for the VM to load the whole memory image, we could create ONE section that spans from the lowest vaddr to the highest vaddr of the sections we want loaded, provided file offsets are contiguous. But the VM specifically looks for section NAMES `.text`, `.data`, `.rodata`. It then loads each by vaddr+offset. So we could:
  - Keep `.text` as-is.
  - Extend `.data` to cover everything after .text + .rodata that we need (vtables, atexit, got, bss...).
  - Keep `.rodata` as-is.
  - Remove/rename `.got`.
  - Optionally extend `.data` size to cover `.bss` (NOBITS) — not needed since memory defaults to zero.

But there's an ordering issue: .rodata (0x4b3d50) sits between .text (0x400240..0x4b2dd0) and .data (0x4fbb90). .rodata is loaded separately by the VM)Skip. So we need:
- .text loaded (0x400240, size 0xb2b90)
- .rodata loaded (0x4b3d50, size 0x31990)
- .data extended to cover 0x4fbb90 .. 0x50d6d0 (or wherever the last alloc section ends), with contiguous file bytes.

But .data's file offset is 0xebb90 and its size would be 0x11b40 (to cover through .got end at 0xfd6cc). Are the file bytes between .data's offset and .got's end contiguous in file? Yes: .data off 0xebb90, __libc_IO_vtables off 0xfb4d0, __libc_atexit off 0xfb818, __libc_subfreeres off 0xfb81c, .got off 0xfb840 size 0x1e8c → end at 0xfd6cc. So contiguous. But the section headers would overlap: if we extend .data to cover vtables' vaddr... the next section headers (__libc_IO_vtables etc.) would overlap with new .data. Overlapping sections are fine for loaders but readelf might complain. Actually the VM only iterates sections by name, so overlapping is harmless. But we should probably remove/zero-out those section headers to avoid confusion? Simplest: keep all section headers but mark the "absorbed" ones as SHT_NOBITS or type 0 (NULL) so the VM's `s.type===1` filter (in vm_test versions) or names don't pick them up. `_gp = 0x513830` in the final binary! Interesting: so `_gp` is defined at 0x513830 in the final linked binary (not 0x11337c). The main prologue `lui gp,0x11; addiu gp,gp,0x337C` + t9→ gp = 0x513830 when t9 = main = 0x4004B4: 0x11337C + 0x4004B4 = 0x513830. And _gp symbol = 0x513830. So gp should be 0x513830 for main. That matches my earlier calculation. 

Wait, so `_gp` in the final binary is 0x513830 (the absolute gp base), and the object code computes gp = _gp + t9 using a pc-relative _gp_disp trick. In the final binary, the linker rewrote `lui gp, hi16; addiu gp, lo16` to encode the difference so that gp + t9 lands on _gp. So `_gp = 0x513830` is the actual gp base address.

Given main is at 0x4004B4)SkipDelay, gp must be 0x513830. For the VM t9=0 at entry, we patch main's prologue to `lui gp,0x51; addiu gp,gp,0x3830` so gp = 0x513830. And `addu gp,gp,t9` adds 0 (t9=0) — fine.

Similarly `__start` at 0x4004F0: prologue lui 0x11, addiu 0x3340 → gp = 0x113340 + 0x4004F0 = 0x513830 too. Good, consistent.

Now let's note: function-specific prologues compute the same _gp (0x513830) regardless of function address — the lui/addiu differ per function to compensate for the different address. Great.

So the two fixes for the current binary:
1. Patch main prologue at 0x4004B4/0x4004B8 to set gp = 0x513830 directly.
   - Original: `3c1c0011` (lui gp,0x11) at 0x4004B4; `279c337c` (addiu gp,gp,0x337C) at 0x4004B8.
   - New: `3c1c0051` (lui gp,0x51) at 0x4004B4; `279c3830` (addiu gp,gp,0x3830) at 0x4004B8.
   Wait — but there's also `addu gp,gp,t9` at 0x4004BC (`0399e021`). With t9=0 in the VM, gp stays 0x513830. But if main is ever called from somewhere with t9 set (not in our flow), gp would be computed as 0x513830 + t9 which would be wrong. But the VM never does thatched. Actually wait — in the original ELF, if someone called main normally (via __start), t9 would be loaded with main's address by __start's `lw t9, -32340(gp)`, then `bal main`, and main's prologue adds t9 → correct. But we're patching main to not add t9 — that would break a normal call. But the VM never calls main via __start; it calls main directly with t9=0. So patched main works under the VM. Fine.

2. Absorb .got into .data so the VM loads it.
